## Order of the Half Moon

The Order of the Half Moon, also known as the Knights of the Half Moon, was a religious order devoted to Selûne and part of the priesthood at the House of the Moon in Waterdeep in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Membership
They were composed of crusaders and it was rumored they had at least one werebear member.

Possessions

The Knights of the Half Moon were distinctively attired in blue-tinted steel skullcaps and sea-blue full-length robes with long collars draping below the shoulders and hemmed with a ring of embroidered full-moon designs. In addition, each member had a tattoo of a crescent moon on their forehead. They were also armed with "moonpoles"—unique polearms topped with crescent-moon blades similar to bardiches.

Activities
They defended the House of the Moon, with six-strong patrols of the temple passing some areas two or three times an hour and groups of two or three standing guard constantly in the Entrance Hall, Narthex, the East and West Guard Towers, and particularly in the Chamber of the Shard, which held the greatest artworks.

Base of Operations
Based at the House of the Moon, the members and their commanders lived within the dormitories of the Garrison of the Half Moon on the third floor at the rear of the temple.

History
The Order of the Half Moon was originally founded to guard the High House of Stars, the first temple to Selûne in Waterdeep that was opened in the . However, in the, Sharran cultists infiltrated the High House of Stars via its cellars from Undermountain, killed most of the priests, looted the vaults, and burnt it down. They even framed followers of Bane for the crime. Thereafter, the Knights of the Half Moon considered this a stain on their honor and they still sought atonement by 1370 DR.

During the Time of Troubles of the Year of Shadows, 1358 DR, a purported avatar of Selûne established herself in the House of the Moon. When confronted by Luna, the temple guards took Luna captive and beat Vajra Valmeyjar. A dozen nights later, while looking for Luna, the adventurer Onyx the Invincible infiltrated the temple and fought with the two Knights of the Half Moon on duty at the doors in the Entrance Hall, before letting in Timoth Eyesbright and the two overcame the Knights. The pair stole their priestly robes and tried to pass themselves off as new converts when called out by two other Knights, Uton and another. But unconvinced, they alerted high priestess Naneatha Suaril, who confronted them while Uton and the other took back their robes. She left them to be dealt with by hell hounds instead.

Members
 Uton

Appendix
Background
The temple guards appearing in the comic "Lunatics" are not named, but the description of the Knights of the Half Moon given in the later Powers & Pantheons makes it clear they are meant to be the same. The guards appearing in the earlier issue "Selune Rising" wear very different armor, so they are assumed to not be of the Order of the Half Moon.

Appearances
 "Lunatics"

## Oral Histories of Faerûn

Oral Histories of Faerûn was a lore book that collected stories and sagely musings on a variety of different topics, published before 1492 DR.

Description
This tome was a thin book with a gold-trimmed cover. Numerous scraps of paper with previous owners' notes and annotations could be found inside.

Content
Chapter 4: Gith and Mind Flayers
Oral Histories of Faerûn: Gith and Mind Flayers was authored by a writer who spoke with Pallidor the Swift, a 700-year-old wood elf from the Wood of Sharp Teeth. The chapter told about the author earning the old elf's trust in order to record his tales. The following poem was told by a warm campfire and Pallidor swore the story was true.

Chapter 7: Paladin Oathbreakers

Oral Histories of Faerûn: Paladin Oathbreakers was written by a writer who spoke with a human paladin oathbreaker, Valemor the Outcast.

Chapter 25: The Spellplague
Oral Histories of Faerûn: The Spellplague was written by a dragonborn author who spoke to two halfling brothers from Luiren, Cornelius and Tomelius Worthywart. They spoke of the loss of their homeland during the catastrophic Spellplague, debating whether it was consumed in blue fire or sunk beneath the waters of the Great Sea.

History
In 1492 DR, copies of this book could be found throughout the Western Heartlands.

## Orca (ship)

The Orca was a ship that sank at the Tower of Storms in the late 15 century DR.

Description
The ship was about  long and had a single mast that stood over  high, which was all that was visible of the ship above water after it was wrecked. It lay just on the southern edge of the rocky outcropping on which the Tower of Storms was perched.

History
The Orca was sunk when its crew was compelled to steer the ship onto the rocks beneath the Tower of Storms by the magic beacon of the lighthouse set by Moesko, an anchorite of Talos. The crew perished, and their bodies were soon picked clean by hungry fish. The wreck then came to be prowled by sharks, including the fearsome Daggermaw.

The plot to sink the Orca was in service to efforts by the Cult of Talos to plunder ships along the Sword Coast North in the late 15 century DR. However, a chest containing a wand of secrets and a spellbook were never recovered from the wreck.

## Orim Redbeard

Orim Redbeard was an infamous pirate captain of the Black Dragon from Konigheim in the Utter East in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Orim was a giant, fat, heavily muscled human with a red beard, hence his nickname. In truth, he had a white beard that he dyed with a mixture of rust and milk.

Personality
Orim was confident in his skills and had a cruel side.

Relationships
Orim hated the Kissing Shark and its captain, Blackfingers Ralingor, and his navigator Drethil.

History
In the, and his crew managed to the destroy the Kissing Shark in a blast of smoke powder near the Tenteeth Point thanks to his hired sorcerer. However, when he heard that some of the surviving Sharkers boarded the Morning Bird, he and his crew hunted that ship in order to find if his hated enemies had survived. Orim's ship attacked the Morning Bird but after Belmer Droon almost managed to kill the mage, Orim asked to parley. However, Kurthe Lornar, still loyal to his old captain, tried to attack Orim, but Belgin Dree managed to stop Kurthe. Orim Redbeard realized that he pursued the wrong ship, and left the Sharkers in peace.

## Orogoth

Orogoth was one of the most dangerous locations in the High Moor and was known for the presence of black dragons.

Description
Orogoth was a large and sprawling ruined villa that belonged to House Orogoth. It once boasted great towers, but they had long since toppled over.

Tunnels beneath the ruin connected it to the Serpent Tunnels of Najara.

Geography
The ruin was located in the heart of the eastern High Moor about  south of Hammer Hall.

History
The villa was built in  by the Netherese Orogoth family. It served as their headquarters for their dark magical experiments and efforts to gain control over dragons and to assume dragon shape. The villa was the site of the family's creation of the ophidians, as well as of a titanic civil war in  during which the family seemingly wiped itself out.

In the, yuan-ti from Ss'thar'tiss'ssun raided the ruins and accidentally awoke a powerful black dragon known as "the Old One". Despite repeated incursions by the yuan-ti, the ruin henceforth became the home and lair of this Old One and his descendants, who claimed to be the last scions of the ancient Orogoth family.

By the, the Old One had enslaved several tribes of ophidians and had recruited Netherese wizards fleeing the fall of Anauria, and the descendants of these groups would also come to populate the ruined villa. Following another civil war within the family in the, the Old One become a dracolich in the  with the help of the Cult of the Dragon. The family then expelled the Cult, earning themselves another longstanding enemy.

In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the reputation and mystique of Orogoth suddenly exploded. The ruin of became well-known among treasure seekers throughout the Sword Coast, with Amnian adventurers and merchants becoming particularly enamored with tales of hidden caches of gems collected by the powerful ancient family. This fame was such that the ruin was mentioned in every guide to the area, and even children heard tales of it. However, those who visited were killed, sent fleeing, or were transformed into beasts.

As of the late 15 century DR, the Old One and his descendants continued to lair in Orogoth.

Inhabitants

In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the villa was home to the black dracolich Calathanorgoth—better known as The Old One—and his living great-grandson, Wastirek, along with Wastirek's mate Vilholin and their children, Awsidorg, Woklef, Cheleen, and Kasidikal. Additionally, it was home to a growing number of half-dragons who traced their ancestry to Calathanorgoth, thus calling themselves the Scions of the Old One.

Non-dragon inhabitants included human wizards descended from the Netherese recruited by Calathanorgoth centuries prior, many of whom studied ancient magic and collaborated with Halruaas wizards. In addition, the villa was the headquarters of the largest ophidian tribe in the High Moor, appropriately calling themselves the Tribe of the Old One. Owing to experiments with ophidian eggs, House Orogoth created a means to manufacture half-dragon versions of the creatures. As of the 1370s DR, the Tribe of the Old One was led by a half-black dragon ophidian named Abreauth.

## Oromanche

Oromanche was a wealthy merchant family from the walled town of Mosstone that laid along the Trade Way in Tethyr.

Organization
The Oromanche family was known to be bombastic and flamboyant people. They had "dark, lush good looks," were bold and daring, and had whirlwinds of alleged and real romances and trysts around the House's reputation. Maerele Oromanche was the matriarch of the merchant family circa 1449 DR. She was good friends with Elminster Aumar. Maerele Oromanche had three significantly younger siblings involved in the family's trade: Elklund, Imraphar, and Odyle. Other siblings included Olora, Emruelle, and Haladeira. Two more family members of note were Maerele's female cousins Jhassala and Nurimbrue.

Activities
The Oromanches made a fortune by importing a variety of goods from the cities of Baldur's Gate and Waterdeep. All of these goods were exotic and not readily available in Tethyr, allowing the Oromanche merchants to charge exuberant prices. Some of the more prominent goods made available by the Oromanches were liqueurs, wines, cheeses, perfumes, and aromatics.

History
The family rose to prominence in the 15 century DR, and by the, Oromanches had power, wealth, and status. They were rivals of the Alkaether merchant family that rose around the same time, however, neither of the two families had active enemies or ongoing feuded, and their rivalry was casual.

In 1449 DR, a mysterious flying Sword of Spells ended a life of a wizard hired by the Oromanche family. The weapon flew through a slot-like vent and stabbed the wizard in the process of ensorcelling the family's home in Mosstone with protective dweomers. Many similar murders were committed by the Sword of Spells until its reign of terror across the Realms ended in the spring of the  and suddenly as it started.

## Orten Imdrar

Orten Imdrar was the Magister from  to .

Appearance
A middle-aged man, he was burly and had a paunch. His white hair was always untidy, and his face wasn't much prettier.

Abilities
He could cast fly, some form of scrying, and cone of cold. He also knew a spell or had a method for blasting open caverns.

History
Before becoming the Magister, Orten Imdrar was a trail-mage who employed utility spells along trips to protect cargo. At the moment of gaining the office of the Magister, he had been doing a stint working magic in mining, where he accidentally slew Aralagath Tarsil by blowing up a cavern beneath the ground where he was sleeping.

Though he feared the mantle of the Magister had passed on to him only so that he could be sacrificed, Orten Imdrar eventually decided to travel and see the world, visiting Waterdeep, Selgaunt, Calimport, and some other locations. To spread magic, he scried on mages to find good magic, and then scried on apprentices, most often attractive young women, to decide where to best give it.

His career ended at the Border Kingdoms, in High Emmerock; he thought to challenge Scsilda Starshield, the Water Witch, and take the sapphires she kept, as well as her home. Elminster, forbidden to attack him, but dedicated to protecting Scsilda, placed a shield around her that would tear anyone apart if they crossed it; he then teleported a fledgling mage, Qilder Yarven, who was nonetheless carrying seven wands, into the path of Orten Imdrar's incoming cone of cold. Qilder retaliated, and Orten was sent into Elminster's shield; heavily damaged, he was finally slain by Qilder Yarven.

## Orb of power (Nalabukhar)

The orbs of power of the Nalabukharn were fabled devices of great magical power, which Magister Phelandern Ghemelt sought during his time in office, during the early 14 century DR.

Rumors & Legends
Per the legends that Phelandern Ghemelt read at some point prior to, these crystal orbs were once in possession of the Calishite Nalabukharn family. There were supposed to be six of them, hidden throughout Faerûn, and others beyond the Nalabukharn might possess the secrets of their operation.

They not only held powers, but could even learn more powers, and operate as dictated by their owner. Whoever owned them could teleport themselves to their position through a command phrase; the orbs also had the power to drain both life and magic, whether spell or magical abilities, through an extruded crystalline tentacle, on command. These drained powers could then be granted to the wielder.

Legend also held that Alair Nalabukhar yet lived inside one of those orbs, where he retreated when his body became too mangled.

## Orosul's Tower

Orosul's Tower was a small tower located in the city of Archenbridge in Archendale during the mid-to-late 14 century DR. Though the place was abandoned by Orosul in the 1350s DR, no Arkhenfolk stepped foot inside ever since as the powerful mage promised that "doom would befall intruders and fair Archendale both."

Location

The inn was located on the intersection between Drover's Lane and Spindral Street, in the southern part of the city.

Structure
The tower was slender and measured  high. Made of granite, it stood in the center of a walled garden closed by two locked iron gates. Although the general belief was that only Orosul possessed the key to its tower, in reality Vangerdahast Aeiulvana and Cylyria Dragonbreast had spares as well.

By 1370 DR, the unattended garden grew thick with vines creeping up the tower's sides. The garden was home of numerous small animals such as birds, rabbits and squirrels, but no larger beasts were ever witnessed there.

It was unclear whether the tower had three or four floors, as it was dotted by shuttered windows in a seemingly random pattern..

Defenses
The city assigned ten soldiers to keep watch around the tower at all times. Equipped with chainmails, slings, blunt-padded arrows and nets, their role was to prevent anyone from casting spells or using magic items within sight of the tower.

History
Coming from Sembia, the mage Orosul used magic to identify the Swords of Archendale. He made a deal with them that supposedly involved a large sum of money, in exchange for which he would be free to settle in town and do as he pleased without being controlled or watched.

Circa 1350 DR, the mage disappeared from the tower after warning the Swords against anyone entering his abode, promising a terrible doom should this rule be broken.

Rumors & Legends
Successful Adventurers
Although nobody has officially entered the tower since Orosul's disappearance, there were persistent rumors in the Dales that this or that adventurer-mage had.

Scary Stories
Archenfolks, who were known for their dislike of strangers, liked to scare visitors by telling them to look at the tower to see what happened to even well-meaning outlanders who settled in the town.

This practice gave birth to a local idiom, "It is deep in Orosul's Tower," which meant 'It is where you will never find it'.

Floating Bed
The few reliable tidbits of information regarding the interior of the tower were revealed by Orosul's apprentice. She let it be known that the mage possessed a large circular magical floating bed in its lofty chambers, a rumor heartily repeated by a local carpenter.

According to her, the bed was surrounded by a cloud of magical darkness filled with twinkling illusory stars at night, and could split apart to reveal a treasure of money and magical items hidden in an extra-dimensional space "under" it.

Ancient Power Scepter
Also according to Orosul's apprentice, one half of a powerful magical Netherese power scepter was hidden under a cobblestone within sight of the tower.

Inhabitants
 Orosul: A powerful mage and the proprietor of the tower.
 Felsharra of the Four Winds: Orosul's apprentice.

References

## Ord Artemel

## Orcish temple (Bezantur)

The Orcish Temple was a place of worship dedicated to the various deities of the Orc pantheon in Bezantur, Thay. It contained crudely carved stone idols which were said to be ancient depictions of Gruumsh, Bahgtru, Ilneval, Luthic, Shargaas, Yurtrus, and various demigods and tribal heroes of the orcs of Thay.

Location
The temple was located southwest of the center of Bezantur, the so-called "City of a Thousand Temples." It lay just south of the City Tombs.

Structure
It was a three-story tall black building.

Activities
The temple was maintained and attended by the orcish warriors mercenaries who served local Thayan officials. They prayed to the stone idols of their gods and heroes in raucous and violent rituals which often involved the sacrifice of prisoners, often a fellow orc who had committed a crime.

Rumors & Legends
The orcish congregation claimed that when their ceremonies were sufficiently bloody and violent, the idol of Gruumsh himself would come to life and offer counsel and guidance to those orcs who prostrated themselves before him.

In, Iymbryl Alastrarra sent Elminster Aumar to Cormanthor, bearing his own kiira, to deliver it upon his brother Ornthalas. At his home, Ornthalas was summoned to find a man in the likeness of Iymbryl holding his sister, Filaurel Alastrarra, as a shield, before a gathering of his immediate family. After the man released Filaurel, he delivered the kiira unto Ornthalas; it bonded quickly, but painfully.

Once he bore the kiira, Ornthalas was supportive of Elminster being taken before the Coronal so that no stain came upon the honor of their family.

Relationships
Riluaneth often teased if not outright tormented him. He was the heir after the death of his brother, Iymbryl. He was the third son of Namryiitha Alastrarra.

## Orc Road

The Orc Road was the thoroughfare between Amnian cities of Crimmor and Purskul.

Description

The Orc Road was the name given to a patch of the Trade Way in Zehoarastria region between Crimmor and Purskul. Farms, family estates, and small agricultural communities filled the stretch of the road, providing the two cities with grains and produce.

History
The road's name derived from it being used by orcish hoards that descended from the Cloud Peaks several times through Amn's history, including the  and the . These two events lead to a large half-orc population in the town of Purskul and the nearby communities. Orc survivors from the failed invasions were enslaved, and their labor was used to pave and maintain the Orc Road.

Sometime circa the, brutal Lord Alathallgo Gathalynker of the Gathalynker family was murdered and, allegedly, eaten by his orcish slaves on the Orc Road. The Lord's body was hastily buried somewhere along the road. In the, Alathallgo's skull adorned in his ornate enchanted helmet was unearthed, but the rest of magical armor and other wondrous items were still believed lost, and buried along the Orc Road. As of the late 14 century DR, the Gathalynkers continued the search for Alathallgo's magical equipment under cover of night, despite the local government's protests and drovers driving their carriages into excavated holes late into the evening.

Notable Locations
Flagonsrich Estate, known for its large gardens and vineyards to the west. As the Orc Road crossed the estate's grounds, a massive holy symbol of Chauntea constructed out of living multi-colored roses could be seen from afar brain a major landmark of the area.

## Orb of isolation

The orb of isolation was a magical item used in the defense of Crosswinds Keep.

Description
The orb was a glass sphere, glowing blue with magical power.

Powers
With a command word, the orb of isolation hovered in place and sealed the area within  against planar travel, in a manner similar to dimensional lock. The warded area could be shaped so long as it remained within the distance limit. The orb's power prevented the ingress of any creature into the warded area, acting as an invisible and impenetrable barrier. Up to three gaps could be created at the user's option on the barrier: these gaps could be any size from  to .

Portals within the warded area had an even stranger effect; they malfunctioned most of the time, and had a further effect on outsiders, who became unable to wander further than  from a portal through which they had entered the warded area. This effect could be ended for any given outsider with a limited wish, miracle, break enchantment or remove curse powerful enough to cancel the orb's magic, or it could be broken for all of them by shutting down the orb.

History
The orb of isolation was part of the defenses of Crosswinds Keep as of .

Notable Owners
 The Stalwart Lions

## Ormalin Relurvor

Ormalin Relurvor was the brother of Xavander the Great. He embezzled a large part of the treasury of Ravens Bluff in the early 14 century DR.

History
During the, Ormalin and Xavander exiled themselves in the Border Kingdoms, using a volcano Xavander found there to melt the treasure Ormalin embezzled.They minted counterfeit Amnian and Calishite coins with the resulting metals, and defined the basics of the future Realm of the Mount.

Ormalin and Xavander disagreed on the direction their newfound kingdom should take: the former wanted a pompous realm of worldwide importance, while the latter wanted to govern over a secretive nation. During a furious argument among brothers, Ormalin died by falling into a lava rift.

## Orosul's scepter

Orosul's scepters were Netherese power scepters capable of healing wounds and charging depleted magic items.

Description
Orosul's scepters were metal bars around  in length, terminated by small metal balls faceted like gems. Each scepter could be split in two, and the halves could subsequently be fused together again by having them touch each other.

Powers
Complete scepters had several magic properties:
 They could recharge depleted magic items when staying in contact with them for several hours.
 They could heal wounds or regrow missing limbs when touching a creature for long enough. Healing simple wounds could take a couple of hours, whereas regrowing limbs could take up to a tenday.
 Scepters could release offensive magic blasts, usually using fire or lightning elements. Different scepters could produce different blasts, and the only way to determine the power of a specific scepter was to use it.

When split, both halves of a scepter had limited powers, which allowed them to be made whole again:
 The half-scepter would glow with a blue-green hue when within  of its matching half.
 The metal ball would glow with a red hue when touching items or brought into areas bearing a dweomer.

History
Because their original name was lost to the ages, these scepters were named after Orosul, a wizard who owned half a scepter and extensively studied it during the 14 century DR.

Rumors
A half of a scepter was said to be hidden under a cobblestone in Archenbridge, within sight of Orosul's Tower. The other half was for a time in possession of the dracolich Rauglothgor, before being brought to Zhentil Keep.

## Orthul's Notch

Orthul's Notch was a sparsely-populated fishing hamlet on Cape Raeth to the southwest of the Cloakwood.

Description
Orthul's Notch was nestled in a small indent in Cape Raeth's eastern side, facing Cloak Bay. By the 14 century DR, Orthul's Notch was a collection of single-store cottages reinforced with local wood and scavenged marine salvage. Those buildings that remained untouched by ruination and decay were the most defensible homes in the Notch.

The few remaining inhabitants of Orthul's Notch survived through salvaging and fishing in its natural harbor, netting for crabs, silverfins, and laethe, a species of eel-like creatures.

History
By the late 14 century DR, Orthul's Notch, as well as other hamlets of Cape Raeth, were almost completely deserted, inhabited by very few hardy folks who lived on fishing and ship-salvage. At some point before the 14 century DR, most inhabitants of the coastal hamlets fled from pirate raids and Cloakwood monster assaults.

Description
The book began by defining the concepts of goodness and lawfulness. To be 'good', it said, was to be free from evil and to seek peace and strike out at wickedness. It said that the practice of upholding the law was to maintain order over chaos, but was not itself free from corruption. It was only the virtue of justice that remained of importance to the Order of the Gauntlet and its holy warriors. It was justice that was demanded by the deities Helm, Hoar, Torm, and Tyr.

History
In the late 14 century DR, a simple wearer discovered what he called the Crypt of Dragons, not far from town. Orncibl claimed that the vast cavern – the Crypt of Dragons, was filled with piles of gold and gems. Corpses of various mummified dragons were carefully arranged atop the treasure piles. They were surrounded by fields of magical protection – crackling blue magic that immediately killed anyone who was stupid enough to approach the hordes. Two of Orncibl's apprentices suffered that grim fate. The weaver himself disappeared soon after he revealed the cave's existence at a tavern in Hilp. He was believed to had been taken by the dreaded Cult of the Dragon or to be hiding from the sinister organization.

## Orange dragon

Orange dragons were a species of chromatic dragon that inhabited tropical swamps, rivers, and lakes. They were rare crossbreeds from salt dragon and red dragon unions in the "Color Wheel" theory.

Description
Orange dragons were born with brilliant orange scales and most retained this coloring into adulthood, with some developing splotches of more reddish or yellowy coloring. Their scales did grow larger and thicker as they aged. Adult orange dragons were typically around  long.

Personality
Orange dragons were highly intelligent but, like all chromatic dragons, evil.

Abilities
Naturally, orange dragons were equally capable on land or in water, and were only slightly less capable in the air. And they were resistant to magical fire and metallic poisons, and immune to natural fire. Their most well-known and deadly ability was their breath weapon: a  stream of liquid sodium (though most only knew it as a silvery metallic liquid) mixed with the dragon's oily saliva. Seconds after they disgorged it, the saliva dried and the sodium burst into flame on contact with air. If the victim tried to rinse the liquid off, they found that it exploded on contact with water, causing even more damage and setting everything in the general vicinity on fire. Only oil could safely "insulate" the liquid sodium from air and water, though that posed major risks as well.

Like other dragons, orange dragons could innately cast spells, gaining pass without trace thrice each day as young, neutralize poison thrice each day as juveniles, raise water twice each day as young adults, heat metal once per day as mature adults, suggestion once per day as very old dragons, and hallucinatory forest once per day as wyrms.

Combat
Orange dragons were well-suited to ambush, but were impulsive enough to be just as likely to attack boldly. One of their preferred tactics was to use their breath weapon on a body of water right beside a camp or boatload of potential prey, blowing the area to smithereens.

Ecology

Orange dragons inhabited tropical forests, where they laired beside and hunted in rivers, lakes, and swamps; accordingly, they were excellent swimmers. They favored their damp environments in part because it meant the fires caused by their breath weapon didn't spread. Within their habitats, they sometimes came into conflict with bronze and black dragons, though black dragons typically preferred to avoid the larger, stronger, and more dangerous orange dragon. In other cases, a black dragon might (grudgingly) accept a subordinate role; orange dragons were known to ally with other evil inhabitants of their territory, with the dragon providing protection and the subordinates providing information and obedience. Bronze dragons, however, typically won otherwise even fights with orange dragons.

Orange dragons preferred to prey on rain forest animals and fish, and enjoyed supplementing their diet with tropical fruits. They enjoyed hunting humans and elves for sport as well as food. If necessary, orange dragons could and would eat insects and fungus. Thanks to their natural resistance to poison (and, presumably, disease), they could drink from pools of water both stagnant and fresh.

This was a solitary species except when mating and raising young, at which times both parents helped protect the nest and hunt for and teach the hatchlings.

Ettercaps were known to prey on hatchling orange dragons and prized their flesh.

History
Dragon crossbreeds that were completely new dragon types, such as the orange dragon in the "Color Wheel" theory (which may or may not be true), were very rare on Toril, as the children of such unions would often take upon characteristics of one parent over another and in other cases were non-dragon anomalies.

## Ornlaugh Turghle

Ornlaugh Turghle was a doppelganger who posed as a merchant in the city of Reddansyr on the Dragon Coast in the late 14 century DR.

Description
In his disguised form, Ornlaugh was a portly man, constantly gasping for air.

History
By the time the doppelganger was discovered, he had impersonated Ornlaugh Turghle for over a decade. As the merchant, Ornlaugh dealt in moneychanging, sold ironmongery, locks, hinges, and fastenings of all types, and ran a merchant fleet. Many of the folk who purchased locking mechanisms from the disguised doppelganger believed that the items were somehow enchanted, allowing doppelgangers to creep into their homes, slay and replace the good folk of Reddansyr. What happened to the real Ornlaugh was unknown, but he was unlikely to have had survived.

## Orin

Orin the Red was a changeling Bhaalspawn and a follower of Bhaal who served her god during the late 15 century DR, by playing a significant role in the Absolute Crisis.

Description
In her true changeling form, Orin had grey-white skin and long blonde hair she kept in a single braid, the end of which was adorned with what resembled the head of a flail. She wore a suit of blood-red armor decorated with a metal belt and wore a circlet atop her head.

Personality
She was one of the most sadistic and bloodthirsty individuals to ever stalk the Sword Coast. Her lust for murder and mayhem surprised even the most ardent devotees of the Lord of Murder. She believed attributing murder to Bhaal had to be done in an artistic manner, not realizing that Bhaal cared not for quality of murder, but of quantity.

Abilities

Like other Bhaalspawn, Orin had the ability to transform into the avatar of Bhaal known as the Slayer.

As a changeling, she also had the ability to take on the appearance of other humanoids, and could deceive even their most loyal allies.

Possessions
Orin performed her killings with two unique blades, the shortsword called Crimson Mischief and the dagger known as the Blade of the First Blood. She also carried with her Bloodthirst, the dagger wielded by the mortal Bhaal when he performed his first murders in the city of Baldur's Gate. She also wore a unique ring that allowed her to retreat from danger.

For a time, she was in possession of one third of the netherstone, which she kept embedded within the Blade of the First Blood.

Relationships
Orin was born to a woman named Helena Anchev, a devoted follower of Bhaal, and Sarevok Anchev, Helena's own father and thus also Orin's grandfather. Sarevok was the child of Bhaal who orchestrated the iron crisis that plagued the Sword Coast in 1368 DR, just prior to the Bhaalspawn crisis that erupted in the Lands of Intrigue. However, Orin was completely unaware of her incestuous origin and the true reason for her mother's attempted murder.Following the murder investigation quest in Act 3 of Baldur's Gate III, there is a dialogue option for the player to declare "Sarevok had a child with his own daughter", that is, an incestuous relationship. The player can learn this by reading "A page from Sarevok's book" which can be found in Sarevok's quarters, or by speaking with Helena's body, telling the truth to Orin, killing Orin, and then speaking with a still-living Sarevok, who reveals it. Since Sarevok is fully human, this implies Helena was a half-doppelganger or changeling. This is also confirmed by an excerpt from Sarevok's memoir where he calls Helena his "shapeshifting beauty".

She herself was served by a cadre of Death's Heads, unholy servants of the Lord of Murder.

Bhaal once dispatched an imp named Cruor to act as her butler in a similar capacity to how Sceleritas Fel served the Dark Urge. However, feeling she didn't need its guidance, Orin killed the imp, but it just came back to life a day later, so she gave it a more painful death. When Cruor returned five days later, Orin made the imp into an effigy, stuffed with the flesh of every butler in Baldur's Gate, as a message to Bhaal that she didn't need a butler.

History

Orin was born from an incestuous union between Sarevok Anchev and his part-doppelganger daughter Helena Anchev, as Sarevok wanted to preserve Bhaal's bloodline and make a sacrifice for Bhaal. When she was aged 8, Helena attempted to murder Orin, which was said by Sarevok to be an attempt to become Bhaal's chosen. To Sarevok's surprise, Orin survived and killed her mother instead. Bhaal manifested and chose Orin as his favorite while Orin toyed with her mother's severed hand.

In her adulthood, Orin was a devoted servant of Bhaal who served directly under the individual known as the Dark Urge during the late 15 century DR. In the, the Dark Urge orchestrated a plot with two fellow chosen of the Dead Three―Lord Enver Gortash and Ketheric Thorm—to rule the Sword Coast in the names of their gods. All the while, Orin bided her time for the right opportunity to take action and seize Bhaal's favor for herself.

After the trio enslaved an elder brain beneath Moonrise Towers with the Crown of Karsus and fabricated the cult of the Absolute religious movement, Orin attacked her superior, inserted a knife into his skull, and implanted a modified illithid tadpole into his brain. She then took over his role as Bhaal's chosen in the plot, but unfortunately proved to be undisciplined. She continued to kill innocents in Baldur's Gate without restraint, seeking to further ingratiate herself to the god Bhaal.

Appendix
Background
As a shapeshifter, Orin's race is fittingly ambiguous. In Baldur's Gate III itself, her race appears to be unspecified, so she could be considered human, appearance notwithstanding, with her shapeshifting power possibly due to being a Bhaalspawn or other magic. However, the Artwork from Baldur's Gate III book describes her thusly: "As a changeling, Orin the Red wields the power to impersonate, transforming in doppelganger guise to deceive her prey." While it could be using 'changeling' as a byword for doppelganger or other shapeshifter as is common in D&D sources, it goes on to give a description matching the first 3.5-edition version of the changeling race, less so the 4- or 5-edition versions. Moreover, while 'changeling' also refers to a child swapped at birth by a fey or doppelganger, Orin's parentage precludes this. Given Sarevok's association with doppelganger agents, it seems likely one of these is intended to be her other grandparent, that is, Helena's mother. In this case, Orin or her grandparent could be a half-doppelganger, as described in Dragon #313, "Strange Bedfellows". Complicating matters, changelings have been changed in each edition, from descendants of doppelgangers in 3.5 edition, another name for doppelgangers in 4 edition, and a separate fey race in 5 edition—any one of these fits Orin. Either way, Orin is expressly of the changeling race, whatever that may be.

## Orpheus

Orpheus, titled the Prince of the Comet and the True Heir, was a githyanki monk purported to be the true ruler of the githyanki people in the late 15 century DR.

History
According to inscribed discs banned by the followers of Vlaakith and found hidden in the Arcane Tower and Crèche Y'llek, Orpheus was the son of Gith, the leader of the rebellion that freed the race that would bear her name from slavery to the mind flayers. Later, learning of how Vlaakith I betrayed Gith and sacrificed her to Tiamat, Orpheus led Gith's Honor Guard in a revolt and named himself the rightful king of the githyanki, triggering the War of the Comet. At the head of the Honor Guard mounted on red dragons, Orpheus bravely fought Vlaakith's knights in a battle in the Astral Plane, where he praised Gith and accused Vlaakith. Yet he was overcome and defeated, lost in the fiery dragon breath of the red ridden by Kith'rak Voss, and was thought dead.

However, Orpheus was spared by Vlaakith and was held captive in a pocket of the Astral Plane that could only be accessed by the Astral Prism, bound with infernal chains that could only be broken by the Orphic Hammer. His Honor Guard remained at his side out of duty as Vlaakith covered up his existence, hoping to one day steal his powers from him while spreading propaganda that Orpheus was a jealous pretender to the throne. Orpheus' imprisonment was only known to Voss, Vlaakith and her descendants, and secret sect of loyal devotees keeping his name alive through githyanki society.

In 1492 DR, Year of the Three Ships Sailing, the elder brain known as the Absolute secretly fed information about the Astral Prism to the Chosen of the Dead Three as part of a calculated plan to escape their control. Out of fear of what Orpheus' powers could do to disrupt their plans, Enver Gortash sent several enthralled illithids in search of the Prism. One such illithid known as the Emperor discovered the Prism first and inadvertently regained its free will. The Emperor then went inside the Prism and discovered Orpheus and proceeded to mentally dominate him to leverage his powers to protect several tadpoled adventurers it directed to oppose the Dead Three and the Absolute.

Description
The githyanki male had a beard and weighed . He wore crimson, purple, and silver attire.

Personality
Like all githyanki, Orpheus harbored a deep hatred of mind flayers. However, he was wise enough to put the liberation of his people first even if meant making certain compromises.

Abilities
Possessing powers similar to those of his mother, Gith, Orpheus was able to disrupt the illithids' hivemind and psionic powers, allowing him to prevent others from becoming illithids by blocking the transformation commands from the Absolute to its modified tadpoles.

## Orlsfall

Orlsfall was a mining town turned ghost town in northern Amn.

Geography
Orlsfall sat below the eastern Cloud Peaks on the north side of the Ridge, not far northwest of the Giants' Dance. It was about two days travel northeast of Amnwater, and an equal distance northwest from Keczulla.

History
The town was named after a heroic miner who saved seven fellow miners before himself being killed in a cave-in. The town grew rapidly in a rush to exploit the mine, and was then abandoned when the mine ran dry. It remained uninhabited until just before 1370 DR, when opportunistic bandits led by Timok the Tusk moved in. Although not quite notorious enough to warrant an assault from nearby Citadel Rashturl, their raids on the nearby Greenfields and growing numbers meant that this was likely to change.

## Orthil Voldovan

Orthil Voldovan was a caravan driver in the Sword Coast during the mid-14 century DR.

Appearance
Voldovan was a hulking, hairy man, with gnarled limbs. His hair was white, and his eyebrows quite bushy as of .

Relationships
Orthil Voldovan was an acquaintance of Rendilar Bluthlock. Tessaril Winter, too, was an acquaintance of his.

He had numerous guards he knew for a long time; they all went by their first name only, with the inexplicable exception of Darhabran Windhome. Though some of Voldovan's guards were Harpers, he wasn't a Harper himself.

Possessions
Voldovan's wagons had a metal flap covering a hole in the ground to let spillages out.

Reputation
Orthil was a "good man", to Tessaril Winter.

History
In, Tessaril Winter instructed Shandril Shessair and Narm Tamaraith to join Voldovan's caravan to make the road from Scornubel to Waterdeep, telling them also that Beldimarr and Arauntar, of his men, were Harpers. Narm and Shandril stuck to the plan while in Scornubel. They were aided in finding him by Marlel, who cautioned them Orthil was leaving on the morning. Once they finally came across Orthil Voldovan, he wanted initially ten gold pieces for Narm and Shandril, but she talked him down to four.

He departed Scornubel with a caravan of thirty-two wagons, plus two "ready-wagons", one wagon for strongboxes and small cargo, and a cook wagon. Orthil was pleased when Shandril and Narm asked to disguise themselves as guards, which they did during the departure from Scornubel. However, even from the start, one of his guards, Storstil, was slain almost as soon as they left Scornubel, and spells in the wagons killed two people. He quickly began to suspect Shandril and Narm at that point, though he had eyewitnesses that placed them away from the murder scenes.

Orthil claimed to be traveling with one mage, but believe there were a dozen more amongst the travelers; rather than find the culprit, he priorized making it to Face Crag before nightfall, which made for a better defensible position. Along the way, they were attacked before making it to the crag.

During the ambush, Voldovan tied up Narm, believing him to have gone out of line by trying to go find Shandril, abandoning his post, rather than protecting the part of the caravan where he had been assigned. Voldovan later approached Shandril, who was with Thorst; he called on her to find some of his healing accoutrements, and ordered Arauntar and Beldimarr, one of which had slain two bandits during the ambush, to drive Thorst's wagon.

Arauntar informed Shandril one morning during the run that she was oversleeping and Orthil was in a rush. Orthil later personally came to demand why she wasn't up. Over some thrusk, Orthil informed them that a number of people had gone missing during the night, and that there were tracks of wild beasts near the caravan, but none of them came to the caravan itself, and also that bandits would almost certainly be shadowing the caravan along the path still. During their conversation Arauntar and Beldimarr turned up and said how they'd arranged for some goods to be moved to the abandoned wagons, still in better shape; Shandril resolved she and Narm would drive on the same wagon, which Beldimarr approved of, claiming they'd be easier to keep track of. Orthil accepted that arrangement.

During that morning, Arauntar and Beldimarr inspected the wagon belonging to Mhegras and Sabran, twice; the second time, they checked on the secret floor they had spotted the first time. Voldovan was summoned for that.  The master of the caravan brought along Shandril and Narm, and the latter checked the contraband horse barding for magic; finding nothing of interest, they left.

After a later ambush, Orthil Voldovan personally sought out Shandril and Narm, and took them to Arauntar and Beldimarr. The latter was about to fulfill a pact with Arauntar, to dispatch him painlessly if mortally wounded; instead, Shandril used her spellfire to heal him. She later used her power to heal Beldimarr; she found the limit of her strength there. Voldovan called afterwards on everyone to hurry to Orcskull Rise.

During the next ambush, after Orcskull Rise, Shandril heard Orthil's expletives to the spell that Aumlar Chauntoun called down, which was rather crude. Orthil Voldovan started considering swording Shandril more seriously after he noticed about a dozen wagons wrecked and on fire at the end of the ambush. Approaching Triel, Voldovan told Darhabran Windhome that he was already willing to kill Shandril.

At Duskview house, Orthil Voldovan and Beldimarr met with herald Stormshield for hiring new sellswords as guards. Briefly, Beldimarr was distracted by another Harper he'd never seen telling him the Three Laws would soon apply on every city; Orthil, after that moment, seemed to have difficulty recognizing Beldimarr, and spoke more stiffly.

Thavaun Impersonation
During the night after leaving Triel, Arauntar and Beldimarr had to protect Shandril and Narm again, as a magic-drinking entity, Evaereol Rathrane, had the caravan in its influence and was blocking both her spellfire and his spells. Sharantyr reached the caravan at this point, and Arauntar took her to be another hunter after spellfire. Only Shandril's intervention prevented them from killing each other; Sharantyr, after that point, said that the man pretending to be Orthil Voldovan wasn't. Beldimarr was then mortally wounded; when Shandril attempted to heal him, she failed, and instead slew him. Orthil ordered all the guards away from Shandril at that point.

Unknown to them, Orthil had been replaced by a red wizard while in Triel: Thavaun. Thavaun had Shandril and Narm sleep in Voldovan's wagon. As Shandril's spellfire went almost completely out of control, the fake Voldovan stabbed Narm from behind, mortally wounding him. Arauntar was the one who finally slew Thavaun, after the latter lowered his guard on watching Shandril disintegrate.

Orthil Voldovan's caravan stopped by Orcskull Rise, using the ridge for protection, along its passage between Scornubel and Triel in late . They had to rush throughout the day to make it, however.

## Orc stone

An orc stone was a magic item that could be used to summon the spirit of an orc in battle.

Description
They were small stones carved with the symbol of an orc tribe, such as the Many-Arrows tribe.

Powers
An orc stone could only be used and attuned to by a person to whom it had been gifted.

On three occasions, an orc stone could be used to summon the spirit of an orc warrior or war chief, which looked exactly as the orc had when they were alive. The spirit would appear within  of the stone's wielder, and would fight on their behalf until it was destroyed or until ten minutes had passed. The spirit was able to speak the same language as the holder of the orc stone, and would obey their orders.

After its third and final use, an orc stone crumbled into dust.

History
One such orc stone was known to contain the spirit of Vokarr the Eyebiter, and found its way to Icewind Dale in the late 15 century DR.

In rare cases, an orc stone might be given as a gift by a member of the Many-Arrows tribe to honor someone who had saved their life. Such a stone would be recognizable by other members of the tribe, who might be willing to parlay with the holder of the stone (or at least with the spirit who inhabited the stone).

The two were known of their explorations of the Shadowfell. Their shred gravestone read: "Adventurers Alexer and Orion Goodbeard - Intrepid explorers of the Plane of Shadows." ## Orc-Slayer (sword)

Orc-Slayer was an enchanted short sword that belonged to Harpo Bogglinn, a hin prince, in the mid–14 century DR.

Powers
The Orc-Slayer blade was ensorcelled to be effective when used against orcs and orckind. When the blade was near an orc, within, the sword shivered as if eagerly excited to shed some orc and orog blood. When used in battle against orckind, the Orc-Slayer's formidable +2 enchantment became a powerful +4 magic that guided the wielder's hand towards the orcish opponents. Each strike against such enemies had its damage magically doubled.

History
In the mid–14 century DR, Price Harpo Bogglinn was exiled from Toril by his father. To redeem himself and return home, the halfling waged a one-hin war against orcs. The Orc-Slayer blade had been used to end the lives of one thousand and one orcs.

## Or-Ghash Gol

High Lama Or-Ghash Gol was a lama dwelling in the Chigidi Mountains on the Plain of Horses. Highly revered, Gol was considered to be the greatest high lama to have ever lived in the area.

History
By, Gol was interred near Mount Or-Ghash.It's not known if the mountain is named for Gol or if Gol is named for the mountain. As a memorial to the high lama, one hundred caskets were placed along the pass to the mountain, approximately  apart. Only one casket held Gol's remains, but which it was was a secret known only to the high lamas dwelling on Mount Or-Ghash. The mountain was visited often by lamas and pilgrims. It was expected that pilgrims make an offering such as a scarf on top of every casket. Strangely, each spring, the high lamas placed a new set of clothes in the true casket of Or-Ghash Gol, and each autumn they took them out again, finding them used, dirtied, and torn.

In the late 15 century DR, Orstrar and Andred made a living by trading in thael leaf (a type of pipeweed flavoring), herbs, and spices, as well as dried foods and fruits, such as apricots. ## Oregano

Oregano was a fragrant plant of wide green leaves and light purple flowers used as a seasoning.

Availability
Oregano was sold as a common seasoning through Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue, priced at two silver pieces per ounce. The same herbs were also sold on the streets of the City of Splendors, Waterdeep.

Oregano was grown in the lush herb gardens of the Sarahin fortress in the Haunted Lands of Zakhara. The aromas of mint, sage, rosemary, dill, parsley, cilantro, thyme, and oregano all clashed with each other and these were ready to be used in the fortress's nearby kitchens.

In the jungles of the Malatran Plateau in Kara-Tur oregano grew near Koshivan and Rudran settlements. The herb was also a known food consumed by the Ancient Nubari. One of the ancient recipes using oregano was discovered and turned into the Zucchini Saiyama style dish.

Some believed that oregano possessed some medicinal properties and received use as a germicide and a pain killer.

In the, alongside Loagranboydar, Wyshbryn, Argrant and Garendor, Orkrash had been given the task of investigating the secret records of noble family trees. ## Orb armor

Orb armor, also known as beholder armor, was the general term for armor used by beholders. Although most beholders found the idea of admitting physical vulnerability or covering their forms abhorrent, some did use orb armor to supplement their survivability.

Description
Orb armor was essentially mundane armor, but designed for beholders. It consisted of a heap of loose armor plates and patches held together with straps, and was much more flexible and articulated than a solid shell of protection. The beholder used their telekinesis eyeray to lift it up and assemble it on their body. It weighed about four times as much and cost about twice as much to make as regular humanoid armor.

## Oracle (spell)

Oracle was an arcane divination spell from the 5 circle of magic used by wizards in the 14 century DR.

Effects
When the spell was cast, it immediately canceled all effects of nearby illusion (or phantasm) magics of the 5 circle of spells and lower. The spell's area of effect was  in radius surrounding the caster, and the caster could pick which effects and targets would be unaffected. This spell negated such magics as reflected image, invisibility, improved invisibility, mirror image, non-detection, and shadow door among others.

The spell was also known to allow the caster to see creatures hidden in the shadows.

Notable Users
 Dinguer the Mad, the duergar spell scroll vendor in the Black Pits gladiatorial arena in the Underdark in the mid–14 century DR.

## Orb of revenant magic

An orb of revenant magic was a type of enchanted orb that could erase certain failures of its wielder.

Powers
Orbs of revenant magic were spellcasting implements that enhanced the lethal effectiveness of arcane and psionic powers. In addition, if the wielder were to miss their foes with such a power, they could call upon the orb once each day to regain their spent energies.

Notable Owners
 The Ice Queen Iyraclea kept an orb of revenant magic in her tower on the Great Glacier.

## Orn

Orn was a fire giant who made his home at Mount Hotenow on the Sword Coast North in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Orn's skin was similar to coal in color. His hair and beard were fiery red. He wore heavy black armor and wielded a giant sword.

Personality
Orn did not care about the consequences of his actions when it came to fulfilling his ambitions of creating a mighty dragon-slaying weapon—including the destruction of the lands around Mount Hotenow.

Activities
Orn took control of Mount Hotenow to harness the powers of the magma river at the mountain's heart. His goal was to create the greatest dragon-slaying weapon the world had ever seen.

History
In attempting to create a weapon using Mount Hotenow's magma river, he caused volcanic activity that threatened surrounding regions. In order to stop him, four adventurers set out and likely defeated him.

## Order of Crimson Warlocks

The Order of Crimson Warlocks was one of the four branches of the Kossuthan clergy at the Flaming Brazier temple in Bezantur, Thay. The Order was created in the mid-to-late 14 century DR to allow the Red Wizards of Thay to formally join the temple in nominal leadership roles.

Organization
The Kossuthan clergy within the Flaming Brazier was traditionally broken up into three branches: the Firewalkers (priests), the Disciples of the Salamander (monks), and the Knights of the Fire Drake (crusaders). Within these branches, hierarchy was very strict. However, Red Wizards who joined the Order of Crimson Warlocks could simply choose any rank within the hierarchy that they personally believed best suited them. This led most of them to choose the highest possible title available: Most Fervid Fire, the second highest rank in the temple, which otherwise denoted the leader of one of the other three branches of the clergy.

Activities
Members of the Order of Crimson Warlocks had no formal duties, and most joined simply for their own amusement or ego rather than out of devotion to Kossuth. While members of the true Kossuthan clergy would outwardly show deference to commands from higher-ranking Order members, they often did not obey these commands without first seeking the approval of their "real" superiors.

History
The Order of Crimson Warlocks was introduced sometime before  by the high priest of the Flaming Brazier, Eternal Flame Mythalanir, who did so in order to build political ties with the Red Wizards. The creation of this fourth branch of the clergy was understood to be a violation of Kossuth's strictures, however it was successful in its goal of pandering to the Red Wizards.

Members
Only Red Wizards or similarly influential Thayan mages—such as nobles or the apprentices of Red Wizards—were permitted to join the Order.

Notable members as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR included:
 Mantorra Hennaflame, a gifted apprentice of Aznar Thrul.
 Grilyanthyn, an elementalist who traveled from the Flaming Brazier to investigate a Kossuthan curse in Procampur.

## Orchtrien

Orchtrien was a gold dragon and the king of Dawnfire during the Time of Dragons, up to the 251 century before DR.

Description
The huge dragon smelled faintly of saffron. When in elven form, he had bronze skin, golden eyes, and blonde hair.

Personality
King Orchtrien was perhaps best defined by a combination of magnanimity, good cheer, extreme overconfidence, and reckless disregard of others' feelings. He was quick to be merciful to traitors, and put his own mercy over the needs and wants of his own closest advisors, duel said advisors almost to death, then consider their own grudge satisfied by his own decree despite denying them a killing blow. He also ignored one of his advisors' confession that he had fallen in love with one of the traitors and his request that the king not involve himself romantically with her, instead sending that advisor in what amounted to a suicide mission, then informing him that he'd taken her as a lover just before the man returned victorious. Even when visiting his own closest advisors turned traitors in a torture chamber, he remained polite, cheerful, kind, and honest, an egregious disconnect.

Abilities
Of note, the dragon had the ability to assume human form, as most of his kind did. He also had the ability to hover in place effortlessly.

He likely had a spell that augmented the flames he could breathe out. Another of his spells prevented extradimensional egress, in the manner of dimensional lock. Yet another of Orchtrien's spells created a dome of rainbows, very much like prismatic sphere, except it could cover a stronghold-sized area, and last all night, probably longer. Other spells of his could animate his breath weapon and make people's blood turn into burning venom within their veins. Finally, one of the dragon's spells created a blazing white mobile flame that could extinguish spells, even a black blade of disaster.

Possessions
The city of Dawnfire was widely seen as being his palace. His laboratories were guarded by at least one ironwyrm golem and one summoned ghargatula. In them, he had an immense collection of texts, spells, and wards, some of which included a treatise on the relationship between dragons and the fundamental forces of the universe.

Activities
Being a King, Orchtrien performed such activities as ruling his subjects and waging war.

Relationships
Orchtrien had a son named Bexendral and a number of other sons, including many "bastards". The half-dragons of Dawnfire were all engendered either by the king or by his sons. His rivals included the red triumvirate. and the green cabal.

Circa ; the elf Rhespen Ash and the human Maldur Breakstone served Orchtrien as his deputies; Rhespen had served the king for over a century as of that date.

History
In −25,090 DR, Orchtrien, already centuries old, was engaged in a number of skirmishes against other dragons, including the red triumvirate and the green cabal; these battles had extended for years at that point, being characterized even by his supporters as "perpetually" and "year after year". While he was in an engagement southeast of Dawnfire, a number of rebellious elven houses set up an ambush for his own soldiers, calling on their aid against trolls. A force led by his deputy Rhespen Ash sallied forth to defend against the trolls, only to get caught in the ambush. His son Bexendral teleported to quell the rebels, saving Rhespen's life. Afterwards, conferring with his deputies, Orchtrien decided to be merciful with the rebels, and have their heirs over as his guests, esquires, and hostages, to better ensure their continuing loyalty. He assigned Rhespen to watch over them and ensure their comfort.

During a feast celebrating a recent victory over the red triumvirate, Orchtrien called on Winterflower Duskmere, one of the hostages, to sing. She sang a song relating the story of a mad king who inflicted calamity on his subjects through misrule; this resulted in his deputy Maldur striking her with a fist spell. She denied any intent to offend, and as the king's deputy Rhespen Ash took her side, Orchtrien commanded that Rhespen and Maldur battle each other for celebratory entertainment, so as to settle the quarrel they'd held for years. Before Rhespen was able to strike the final blow, Orchtrien considered their quarrel over, and bid them work harmoniously together forevermore.

At a later date during or before winter, in preparation for another celebration, this time over the green cabal's warriors, Orchtrien called upon Rhespen Ash to prepare Lady Winterflower to sing again, requesting in advance that the song be less suggestive. During the celebration itself, he commented to Rhespen that he intended to seduce Lady Winterflower, finding the scent of treason in her all the more enticing. He also gave Winterflower a bracelet in the shape of a gold wyrm, wrapped in a spiral shape. After the celebration, Rhespen asked Orchtrien not to seduce Winterflower, and instead seek his entertainment elsewhere, because he was in love with her. Orchtrien agreed to comport himself accordingly.

The king then ordered Rhespen Ash to fight a battle on the lands of the green cabal through the winter months. When they were beset by a green dragon, Rhespen used a sending spell to call for Orchtrien's help, but the dragon didn't respond, as fighting that dragon himself would have escalated the conflict beyond his designs. By his return in early spring, Rhespen Ash had slain that dragon alone. Orchtrien held him in great esteem for that, and had Maldur break him the news, prior to his arrival, that Winterflower was now Orchtrien's own mistress, as well as deliver fresh mounts and clothes. During the celebration itself, Rhespen asked the dragon why he did not respond to the summons. The dragon claimed, falsely, that no message had reached him. Orchtrien then promoted Rhespen to one of his chief marshals.

Orchtrien later learned the newly minted chief marshal had copied his spellbooks, which had been taken by Winterflower before she disappeared. Visiting the captured elf in the torture chamber, the dragon learned Winterflower had made Rhespen believe that the King controlled her mind. He explained that her plan was almost certainly to turn Rhespen against him so he'd slay him somehow, and that she'd convinced Orchtrien himself that she'd fallen for the dragon king, probably to improve her position for spying or a possible regicide. He then decided to leave Rhespen where he was.

The dragon marched his army into the forest where House Duskmere lived, but found their residences empty, requiring a thorough search of the forest for their hideaway. Once the dragon found the hideout, he cast a few spells to prevent their escape, and retrieved his army, which arrived by dusk. He was then confronted by Rhespen Ash, who'd managed to find the stronghold not long before he did, and turned completely over to the rebel cause. Orchtrien prevailed in their combat, the stronghold was sacked, and the books retrieved, save one carried away by Lady Winterflower Duskmere, a treatise on the connection between dragons and the fundamental forces of the world.

In, King Orchtrien was witnessing a comet's passage from his palace when he was struck by a bout of maddened bloodthirst, leading him to attack a flirty courtesan, and then the rest of his palace, before turning on his own army in an orgy of death. As he was slain, Orchtrien realized what he'd done, and only managed to whisper "the red star murdered me." Thus it was known as the King-Killer Star.

Rumors & Legends
Whether she genuinely believed so or not, Winterflower Duskmere claimed that King Orchtrien and his kin were mind-controlling rapists.

## Orgnir

Orgnir the Lost was an undead creature that once was a giant adventurer who perished in the Dragonbone Vale of the Sword Mountains sometime before the late 15 century DR.

History
Orgnir's career as a giant adventurer came to an end when he got lost in the Sword Mountains sometime before the late 15 century DR. Wandering the mountain slopes, he came across the Dragonbone Vale. Exhausted, Orgnir collapsed near the ruin where the Scaleblight Mythallar was buried under. As the giant was sleeping, the mythallar's corruption seeped into his body, stealing the adventurer's life and twisting his now undead form into a guardian bound to the artifact. With years, Orgnir the Lost became a guardian of the lands, guiding the dead dragons to their graves in the valley.

## Ormo

Description
Ormo was human man with light brown eyes and pale skin color. He wore a sturdy set of splint mail armor and a metal helmet. In battle, he wielded a longsword and a simple round shield.

History
In the, Ormo was a member of a gang assembled by Churg Elvek, secretly a cultist of the Absolute, who sought to strengthen its grasp of Baldur's Gate. When the famed writer Volothamp Geddarm returned to the Gate after misadventures in the Emerald Grove and coming face-to-face with the Absolute's army, Volo became a target for Churg and his gang. The master traveler was captured by Ormo and the rest of the thugs and tied to a stack of smokepowder barrels, ready to be "brought to justice" for being an alleged agent of the Absolute at the hands of the gang and street gawkers. When a band known as the Absolute Adversaries interrupted the street execution, Ormo, Churg Elvek, and the rest of the gang attacked the adventurers but were defeated.

## Order of Belox

The Order of Belox, also called House Belox, was an Amnian trading company that operated in Amn's colony at Port Nyanzaru in Chult during the 15 century DR.

Activities
The Order of Belox specialized in the recovery of relics from ancient Serpentes. While some of these artifacts were sent back to Amn as museum pieces, others were stripped of gems and melted down for precious metals to sell.

As an Amnian trading company in Port Nyanzaru, the Order of Belox wielded a great deal of influence in the city and was in constant political and financial competition with the other trade companies. Affiliation with the company granted the right to reside within the safety of Port Nyanzaru's wall.

Possessions
Agents of the Order of Belox had access to old maps that showed the locations of ancient Serpentes temples as well as good paths between them.

History
In the, the Order of Belox participated in sponsoring a great hunt that served as a competition between Port Nyanzaru's dozens of trading companies. They vied against the other companies—notably Charisk Expeditionary Outfitters, House Selemchant, Naelishti's Naturals, and Zazefay Logging and Mining—to recruit the most capable hunters. The company's maps gave their competitors an edge in navigating the jungle.

Amnian companies like the Order of Belox were subsequently expelled from Port Nyanzaru by the Ytepka Society and a local merchant consortium in the late 15 century DR, and were replaced by seven merchant princes as the city's mercantile and political leaders. The Order of Belox's trade in jewels and metals was taken over by the prince Zhanthi while their specialization in maps and relics became the purview of Wakanga O'tamu.

Members
The company's liaison to hired adventurers was a human archeologist who wore spectacles.

## Oriphaun Huntsilver

Oriphaun Huntsilver was a Cormyte nobleman, treasure hunter, adventurer, and Harper agent of House Huntsilver in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Oriphaun was a human man with auburn hair and muttonchops. He carried a longsword.

Personality
Oriphaun went into the world as something of a thrill-seeker, looking for adventure and treasure

History
Shadow Over Wheloon

In the late 15 century DR, Oriphaun decided to become an adventurer, to his uncle Belvedere's disapproval. An aspiring relic collector, he was roaming about Cormyr hunting for rare artifacts when he first laid eyes on a Nether Scroll in the Storm Horns. In his whimsy, he considered it to be a suitable trinket to add to his collection, though he did not manage to actually obtain it at the time and it was instead taken by the forces of Thultanthar, who were also in the area secretly recovering an intact mythallar. His uncle, along with the footman Antone Corluk, traveled to Eveningstar in search of adventurers to bring Oriphaun home, or at least ensure his survival.

After checking with Oversword Edric Crastmere, Oriphaun slipped into the prison city—unbeknownst to both the Shadovar and the Purple Dragons—seeking thrills and interesting trinkets, having bribed the lizardfolk of the underground tunnels to smuggle him in. Unwittingly discovering the Netherese in the process of their invasion, he knew that his duty demanded that he put the good of the realm ahead of his pleasures, and so devoted his efforts towards uncovering how the Shadovar had entered the city and what they hoped to achieve there. Discovering that they had a Nether Scroll in their possession and were using it to gather an army to strike at Cormyr through the Shadowfell, he prepared to steal it from Oldstone Hall when adventurers sent by the Wizard of War Jacinta Last found him at the entrance.

After a brief discussion with the Cormyrean agents about the situation, Oriphaun climbed up the outside of the structure and planned to sneak in through a window where the archwizard Tyvrus kept the Scroll, while Cormyr's forces fought through the manor, in part using the gossamer-thin Shadowfell border to overcome the defenders. The agents arrived at the top of Oldstone Hall's tower only to find Oriphaun already there, frozen in place by Tyvrus's magic and the Nether Scroll in his hand. After a grueling fight with the arcanist in both material and shadow realms, Tyvrus was slain and the Shadowfell Nexus collapsed, ending the Shadowfell connection to Wheloon and stranding the remaining Netherese forces inside the city. Without a moment to waste, Oriphaun took the Scroll away from the city in order to hide it from its former owners.

The High Road of Shadows

Oriphaun fled the scene in Tyvrus's tower as soon as he had the Nether Scroll, knowing that he would be pursued to the death by the agents of Netheril. He managed to make contact with the Harpers and was quickly initiated into the group, then vanished once more into the wilderness. In an attempt to buy time before the shades of Thultanthar recovered the Scroll, or at least prevent them from getting the whole thing, he tore it into several pieces and hid them in various places along the High Road between Eveningstar and Tyrluk, hoping that his Harper allies could muster their forces in time to stop the Netherese properly. With his part done to the best of his ability, Oriphaun moved on as quickly as possible to return to the Storm Horns, where he overcame a Shadovar spellcaster and captured their staff. Realizing that the staff was a powerful and dangerous artifact, Oriphaun dropped it off with his friend Halsaime while he pursued his investigations.

The Secret of the Storm Horns

Oriphaun went on to discover a Netherese plot to raise a flying enclave from a mountain glacier in the Storm Horns, using the the power of an unearthed mythallar, and sent an urgent request to the Harpers for backup to meet him in the Yarrow Glade. However, when the adventurers met with him they were ambushed by Netherese forces led by Archwizard Amskar, who captured Oriphaun and brought him to the glacier. Oriphaun was later found and freed by the adventurers as the grand scheme reached its completion. Though they were unable to prevent Amskar from reactivating the mythallar and raising the glacier, they broke the enclave's flight pillars and killed Amskar before jumping off as the enclave flew out of control and smashed into a mountainside, ending the Netherese invasion plans. After the successful thwarting of the Netherese conspiracies, Oriphaun returned safely to his family.

## Orluth Angalaer

Orluth Angalaer was one of the Magelords of Athalantar in the early 3 century DR.

Description
He had dark robes and an elegant hand.

Personality
The man was supremely overconfident.

Abilities
He strongly implied at least one of his spells responded to attacks.

History
After the elimination of the Velvet Hands gang of Athalantar in the, some of the Moonclaws, a rival gang, were captured by the Magelords. The captured Moonclaws told the Magelords about the preferred hideout of the Velvet Hands. One of the magelords, Orluth Angalaer, decided to lie in wait there, expecting that any surviving Velvet Hands wouldn't return for at least one day.

He was in wait when Elminster Aumar, one of the thieves of the Velvet Hands, arrived at the hideout. He tried to taunt him into attacking him so as to try out a spell, but Elminster instead triggered an enchanted ring akin to a ring of the ram that he inherited from a fellow Velvet Hand, Tassabra. The strike hurled Orluth out the window, and impaled him on the poles of a fish cart, killing him.

## Oryne Belamadin

Oryne Belamadin was an Amaunatori priestess in Cormyr during the mid-1480s DR.

Description
Oryne had light skin and golden hair in twin braids.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion, Oryne aided the defenders of Eveningstar. She used spies and divination to learn about the masters of the invasion, House Szind, and discovered that they were on the verge of a necromantic revolution—powerful, intelligent undead that lost none of their living faculties and were entirely loyal to their creators. She quickly organized a strike team to eliminate this threat in particular and weaken Szind and the drow more generally, eschewing all subtlety in favor of simply destroying everything while using drow-seeming illusory disguises to implicate rival houses. She instructed them to use captured flashstones to destroy the special necromantic cauldrons while eliminating the house's necromancers and extant undead.

The Cormyrean team managed to fulfil the objectives, wrecking the estate and eliminating the lead necromancer Relig, and possibly slaying Matron Zhivsha Szind. In the end it may have merely been Relig's overly-grandiose pronouncements that drew Oryne's notice in the first place, with the ultimate undead being nothing more than a phantom menace.

## Orthalar

Orthalar of the Winds was a mage from Calimport in the early-to-mid 3 century DR. He was hired by Felodar Aumar to seize control of Athalantar.

Personality

Abilities
He was a master of magics that hurled wind as a weapon, and was an assiduous user of contingency and escape spells.

Possessions
The wizard owned a home in Hastarl, Athalantar.

Activities
Orthalar was one of the Night Mages that worked by night to try to seize control of Athalantar for Felodar Aumar, whilst hiding from the Magelords of Athalantar during the day.

History
Orthalar came to Athalantar at some point during the reign of King Belaur Aumar, having been sent by the king's brother Felodar as part of an effort to seize control of the realm. During his time in Athalantar, he survived at least three attacks by the Magelords. In the, presumably before the coup by Prince Elminster Aumar, Orthalar lured five Magelords to his house and trapped them all in the cellar, where he likely died with them.

However, there was no hard evidence he had died; the chance remained that he might have simply faked his death.

## Order of the White Crane

The Order of the White Crane was a monastic order in Shou Lung.

Dogma
The sect was devoted to the Path of Enlightenment and to Shu Chia, one of the Nine Immortals and the God of Artificers and the Arts. It was a conservative sect, but nonetheless had a dogma of versatility.

Activities
In addition to their religious study and practices and their martial arts training, the White Crane monks trained as astrologers, craftsmen, herbalists, historians, and physicians. They focused on religious devotions, martial arts training, and manual labor in equal measure.

They freely allowed travelers to stay at their temple, but could share only a simple sleeping mat and vegetarian food, and anyone staying for more than a week was expected to work and earn their keep. Visitors were also permitted to attend their training.

Tactics
They practiced the White Crane style of kung fu, a martial art that emphasized swiftness and grace. Sohei of the order were skilled in wielding two matching weapons at once: butterfly blades, jitte, nunchaku, or sai.

Base of Operations
They were based in the White Crane Temple in the city of Kirin in Hungtse Province.

Members
Circa, the order was led by the abbot Ch'ang Tui, "Long Legs", who upheld its traditions.

History

During the Darkening of Faerûn, Orrex was one of the leading Silver Flame investigators who pursued the Lords of Dust in Stormreach as they attempted to break into the Spinner's Prison and free the Spinner of Shadows. Despite the best efforts of the Church of the Silver Flame and their allies, the Spinner escaped into the Demonweb Pits thanks to the intervention of Lolth, who broke into the prison herself and opened the Rift Between Worlds. Following this, Orrex called in the Gatekeepers to assist, the experts on planar gateways.

## Orin Arroway

Swordcaptain Orin Arroway was a Purple Dragon of Cormyr, who was stationed in the town of Eveningstar around the time of the invasion from Sschindylryn in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Orin was a big man with short white hair. He wore a suit of chain mail.

History
In the mid-1480s DR Orin served in the Purple Dragons, and was deployed in Eveningstar. When Storm Horns orcs came down attempted to raid the village, the attack was defeated and exposed Amskar's operation in the process. While others believed it to be a random attack, Orin agreed with the Harpers Kara Patwin and Lorant Tass that something was going on in the mountains and needed to be investigated. While he was forbidden from sending the Purple Dragons to investigate in the wilderness, he did have the ability to hire and organize outsiders to do the same thing, leading to him sending an attack on the Netherese central camp where the core of the Thultanthar-allied army was based.
